27. namespace SDKTemplate
28. {
29.     /// See the README.md for discussion of this scenario.
30.     public sealed partial class Scenario2_EventHandlers : Page
31.     {
32.         private BitrateHelper bitrateHelper;
33. 
34.         public Scenario2_EventHandlers()
35.         {
36.             this.InitializeComponent();
37.         }
38. 
39.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
40.         {
41.             // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
42.             ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
43.             var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;
44.             if (mediaPlayer != null)
45.             {
46.                 mediaPlayerLogger?.Dispose();
47.                 mediaPlayerLogger = null;
48. 
49.                 UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayer);
50. 
51.                 mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(null);
52.                 mediaPlayer.Dispose();
53.             }
54.         }
55. 
56.         private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
57.         {
58.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
59.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
60.             if (mpItem != null)
61.             {
62.                 adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
63.             }
64.             MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
65.             if (source != null)
66.             {
67.                 adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
68.             }
69. 
70.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
71.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;
72. 
73.             mediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
74.             mediaSourceLogger = null;
75. 
76.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
77.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = null;
78. 
79.             UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
80.         }
81. 
82.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
83.         {
84.             // Explicitly create the instance of MediaPlayer if you need to register for its events
85.             // (like MediaOpened / MediaFailed) prior to setting an IMediaPlaybackSource.
86.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
87. 
88.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
89.             mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);
90. 
91.             // Ensure we have PlayReady support, in case the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box.
92.             var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
93.             prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);
94.             mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);
95. 
96.             // This Scenario focuses on event handling, mostly via the "Logger" helper classes
97.             // in the Shared folder.
98.             //
99.             // In addition, the App can also insert an IHttpFilter into the Windows.Web.Http stack
100.             // which is used by the AdaptiveMediaSource.  To do so, it must derive a class from
101.             // IHttpFilter, provide the HttpBaseProtocolFilter to that class to execute requests,
102.             // then use the derived filter as the constructor for the HttpClient.
103.             //
104.             // The HttpClient is then used in the constructor of the AdaptiveMediaSource.
105.             // The App should explicitly set:
106.             //   CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache
107.             // When passing an HttpClient into the constructor of AdpativeMediaSource,
108.             // set this on the HttpBaseProtocolFilter.
109.             //
110.             // More than one IHttpFilter can be nested, for example, an App might
111.             // use a filter to modify web requests: see Scenario3 for details.
112.             //
113.             // In this scenario, we add an AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger to provide additional
114.             // verbose logging details for each request.
115.             var baseProtocolFilter = new HttpBaseProtocolFilter();
116.             baseProtocolFilter.CacheControl.WriteBehavior = HttpCacheWriteBehavior.NoCache;  // Always set WriteBehavior = NoCache
117.             var fistLevelFilter = new AdaptiveMediaSourceHttpFilterLogger(LoggerControl, baseProtocolFilter);
118.             var httpClient = new HttpClient(fistLevelFilter);
119. 
120. 
121.             ContentSelectorControl.Initialize(
122.                 mediaPlayer,
123.                 MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
124.                 httpClient,
125.                 LoggerControl,
126.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
127.         }
128. 
129. 
130.         #region Content Loading
131. 
132.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
133.         {
134.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
135. 
136.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
137.             if (httpClient != null)
138.             {
139.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
140.             }
141.             else
142.             {
143.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
144.             }
145. 
146.             MediaSource source;
147.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
148.             {
149.                 var adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
150. 
151.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
152.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
153. 
154.                 // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
155.                 RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
156. 
157.                 // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
158.                 bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);
159. 
160.                 // The AdaptiveMediaSource chooses initial playback and download bitrates.
161.                 // See the Tuning scenario for examples of customizing these bitrates.
162.                 await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
163.                 await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);
164. 
165.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
166. 
167.             }
168.             else
169.             {
170.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
171. 
172.                 // In this scenario, we make a second attempt to load any URI that is
173.                 // not adaptive streaming as a progressive download URI:
174.                 Log($"Attempting to create a MediaSource from uri: {uri}");
175.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromUri(uri);
176.             }
177. 
178.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
179.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
180. 
181.             // You can save additional information in the CustomPropertySet for future retrieval.
182.             // Note: MediaSource.CustomProperties is a ValueSet and therefore can store
183.             // only serializable types.
184. 
185.             // Save the original Uri.
186.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
187. 
188.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
189.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
190. 
191.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
192. 
193.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
194.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
195. 
196.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
197. 
198.             return mpItem;
199.         }
200. 
201.         private async void HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen()
202.         {
203.             // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.
204.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
205.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
206.             {
207.                 DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
208.             });
209.         }
210.         #endregion

215.         private void RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
216.         {
217.             adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged += DownloadBitrateChanged;
218.             adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged += PlaybackBitrateChanged;
219.         }

221.         private void UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource)
222.         {
223.             if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
224.             {
225.                 adaptiveMediaSource.DownloadBitrateChanged -= DownloadBitrateChanged;
226.                 adaptiveMediaSource.PlaybackBitrateChanged -= PlaybackBitrateChanged;
227.             }
228.         }
229.         private async void DownloadBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourceDownloadBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
230.         {
231.             uint downloadBitrate = args.NewValue;
232.             await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(downloadBitrate);
233.         }
234. 
235.         private async Task UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(uint downloadBitrate)
236.         {
237.             // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
238.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>
239.             {
240.                 iconDownloadBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(downloadBitrate);
241.                 txtDownloadBitrate.Text = downloadBitrate.ToString();
242.             }));
243.         }
244. 
245.         private async void PlaybackBitrateChanged(AdaptiveMediaSource sender, AdaptiveMediaSourcePlaybackBitrateChangedEventArgs args)
246.         {
247.             uint playbackBitrate = args.NewValue;
248.             await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(playbackBitrate);
249.         }
250. 
251.         private async Task UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(uint playbackBitrate)
252.         {
253.             // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
254.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, new DispatchedHandler(() =>
255.             {
256.                 iconPlaybackBitrate.Symbol = bitrateHelper.GetBitrateSymbol(playbackBitrate);
257.                 txtPlaybackBitrate.Text = playbackBitrate.ToString();
258.             }));
259.         }

39.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
40.         {
41.             // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
42.             ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
43.             var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;
44.             if (mediaPlayer != null)
45.             {
46.                 mediaPlayerLogger?.Dispose();
47.                 mediaPlayerLogger = null;
48.

50.

52.                 mediaPlayer.Dispose();
53.             }

56.         private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
57.         {
58.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
59.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
60.             if (mpItem != null)
61.             {
62.                 adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
63.                 UnregisterForMediaSourceEvents(mpItem.Source);
64.                 UnregisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(mpItem);
65.             }
66.             MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
67.             if (source != null)
68.             {
69.                 adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
70.                 UnregisterForMediaSourceEvents(source);
71.             }
72. 
73.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
74.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;
75. 
76.             mediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
77.             mediaSourceLogger = null;
78. 
79.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
80.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = null;
81. 
82.         }

84.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
85.         {
86.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
87. 
88.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
89.             mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);
90. 
91.             // Ensure we have PlayReady support, in case the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box.
92.             var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
93.             prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);

95.

97.                 mediaPlayer,

99.                 null,
100.                 LoggerControl,
101.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
102.         }

107.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
108.         {
109.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
110. 
111.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
112.             if (httpClient != null)
113.             {
114.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
115.             }
116.             else
117.             {
118.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
119.             }
120. 
121.             MediaSource source;
122.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
123.             {
124.                 var adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
125. 
126.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
127.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
128. 
129.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
130. 
131.             }
132.             else
133.             {
134.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
135.                 return null;
136.             }
137. 
138.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
139.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
140. 
141.             // Save the original Uri.
142.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
143. 
144.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
145.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "MainContent_ID";
146. 
147.             // In addition to logging, this scenario uses MediaSource events:
148.             RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(source);
149. 
150.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
151. 
152.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
153.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
154. 
155.             // In addition to logging, this scenario uses MediaPlaybackItem events:
156.             RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(mpItem);
157. 
158.             // Now that we should have a MediaPlaybackItem, we will insert pre- mid- and post-roll ads
159.             // with the MediaBreak API.
160.             CreateMediaBreaksForItem(mpItem);
161. 
162.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
163. 
164.             return mpItem;
165.         }
166. 
167.         private async void HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen()
168.         {
169.             // On small screens, hide the description text to make room for the video.
170.             // TODO[migrate-015]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
171.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
172.             {
173.                 DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
174.             });
175.         }
176. 
177.         private void CreateMediaBreaksForItem(MediaPlaybackItem item)
178.         {
179.             if (item != null)
180.             {
181.                 // We have two ads that will be repeated.
182.                 var redSkyUri = new Uri("http://az29176.vo.msecnd.net/videocontent/RedSky_FoxRiverWisc_GettyImagesRF-499617760_1080_HD_EN-US.mp4");
183.                 var flowersUri = new Uri("http://az29176.vo.msecnd.net/videocontent/CrocusTL_FramePoolRM_688-580-676_1080_HD_EN-US.mp4");
184. 
185.                 // One option is to create a separate MediaPlaybackItem for each of your ads.
186.                 // You might choose to do this if each ad needs different reporting information.
187.                 // Another option is to re-use MediaPlaybackItems in different MediaBreaks.
188.                 // This scenario demonstrates both patterns.
189.                 var ad1 = new MediaPlaybackItem(MediaSource.CreateFromUri(redSkyUri));
190.                 ad1.Source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "Ad1_ID";
191.                 ad1.Source.CustomProperties["description"] = "Red Sky";
192.                 ad1.Source.CustomProperties["uri"] = redSkyUri.ToString();
193.                 RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(ad1.Source);
194.                 RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(ad1);
195. 
196.                 var ad2 = new MediaPlaybackItem(MediaSource.CreateFromUri(flowersUri));
197.                 ad2.Source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "Ad2_ID";
198.                 ad2.Source.CustomProperties["description"] = "Flowers";
199.                 ad2.Source.CustomProperties["uri"] = flowersUri.ToString();
200.                 RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(ad2.Source);
201.                 RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(ad2);
202. 
203.                 var ad3 = new MediaPlaybackItem(MediaSource.CreateFromUri(redSkyUri));
204.                 ad3.Source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = "Ad3_ID";
205.                 ad3.Source.CustomProperties["description"] = "Red Sky 2";
206.                 ad3.Source.CustomProperties["uri"] = redSkyUri.ToString();
207.                 RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(ad3.Source);
208.                 RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(ad3);
209. 
210.                 // Create a PrerollBreak on your main content.
211.                 if (item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak == null)
212.                 {
213.                     item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak = new MediaBreak(MediaBreakInsertionMethod.Interrupt);
214.                 }
215. 
216.                 // Add the ads to the PrerollBreak in the order you want them played
217.                 item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(ad1);
218.                 item.BreakSchedule.PrerollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(ad2);
219. 
220.                 // Add the ads to the MidRoll break at 10% into the main content.
221.                 // To do this, we need to wait until the main MediaPlaybackItem is fully loaded by the player
222.                 // so that we know its Duration. This will happen on MediaSource.OpenOperationCompleted.
223.                 item.Source.OpenOperationCompleted += (sender, args) =>
224.                 {
225.                     var attachedItem = MediaPlaybackItem.FindFromMediaSource(sender);
226.                     if (sender.Duration.HasValue)
227.                     {
228.                         // For live streaming, the duration will be TimeSpan.MaxValue, which won't work for this scenario,
229.                         // so we'll assume the total duration is 2 minutes for the purpose of ad insertion.
230.                         bool isLiveMediaSource = item.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource != null ? item.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource.IsLive : false;
231.                         long sourceDurationTicks = isLiveMediaSource ? TimeSpan.FromMinutes(2).Ticks : sender.Duration.Value.Ticks;
232.                         var positionAt10PercentOfMainContent = TimeSpan.FromTicks(sourceDurationTicks / 10);
233. 
234.                         // If the content is live, then the ad break replaces the streaming content.
235.                         // If the content is not live, then the content pauses for the ad, and then resumes
236.                         // after the ad is complete.
237.                         MediaBreakInsertionMethod insertionMethod = isLiveMediaSource ? MediaBreakInsertionMethod.Replace : MediaBreakInsertionMethod.Interrupt;
238.                         var midRollBreak = new MediaBreak(insertionMethod, positionAt10PercentOfMainContent);
239.                         midRollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(ad2);
240.                         midRollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(ad1);
241.                         attachedItem.BreakSchedule.InsertMidrollBreak(midRollBreak);
242.                         Log($"Added MidRoll at {positionAt10PercentOfMainContent}");
243.                     }
244.                 };
245. 
246.                 // Create a PostrollBreak:
247.                 // Note: for Live content, it will only play once the presentation transitions to VOD.
248.                 if (item.BreakSchedule.PostrollBreak == null)
249.                 {
250.                     item.BreakSchedule.PostrollBreak = new MediaBreak(MediaBreakInsertionMethod.Interrupt);
251.                 }
252.                 // Add the ads to the PostrollBreak in the order you want them played
253.                 item.BreakSchedule.PostrollBreak.PlaybackList.Items.Add(ad3);
254.             }
255.         }
256.         #endregion

258. 
259.         #region MediaSource Event Handlers
260. 
261.         private void RegisterForMediaSourceEvents(MediaSource source)
262.         {
263.             source.OpenOperationCompleted += Source_OpenOperationCompleted;
264.         }
265.         private void UnregisterForMediaSourceEvents(MediaSource source)
266.         {
267.             source.OpenOperationCompleted -= Source_OpenOperationCompleted;
268.         }
269. 
270.         private void Source_OpenOperationCompleted(MediaSource source, MediaSourceOpenOperationCompletedEventArgs args)
271.         {
272.             // Here we create our own metadata track.
273.             // Our goal is to track progress through the asset and report it back to a server.
274.             object customValue = null;
275.             source.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("contentId", out customValue);
276.             string contentId = (string)customValue;
277.             var reportingUriFormat = "http://myserver/reporting/{0}?id={1}";
278.             // From \Shared\TrackingEventCue.cs:
279.             TrackingEventCue.CreateTrackingEventsTrack(source, contentId, reportingUriFormat);
280.         }
281. 
282.         #endregion
283. 
284. 
285.         #region MediaPlaybackItem Event Handlers
286. 
287.         private void RegisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(MediaPlaybackItem item)
288.         {
289.             // If we encountered some unknown tags in an m3u8 manifest,
290.             // we might already have metadata tracks in the source,
291.             // which is used as the constructor for the MediaPlaybackItem.
292.             // Therefore, the MediaPlaybackItem may already have metadata tracks.
293.             // Since TracksChanged will not be raised for these it is best to check
294.             // if we have tracks, and set up the handlers:
295.             if (item.TimedMetadataTracks.Count > 0)
296.             {
297.                 Log($"Registering existing TimedMetadataTracks, Count: {item.TimedMetadataTracks.Count}");
298. 
299.                 for (int index = 0; index < item.TimedMetadataTracks.Count; index++)
300.                 {
301.                     RegisterMetadataHandlers(item, index);
302.                 }
303.             }
304. 
305.             // For any tracks found as the content is parsed, we register for the event handler:
306.             item.TimedMetadataTracksChanged += Item_TimedMetadataTracksChanged;
307.         }
308. 
309.         private void UnregisterForMediaPlaybackItemEvents(MediaPlaybackItem item)
310.         {
311.             item.TimedMetadataTracksChanged -= Item_TimedMetadataTracksChanged;
312.             foreach (var track in item.TimedMetadataTracks)
313.             {
314.                 UnregisterMetadataHandlers(track);
315.             }
316.         }
317. 
318.         private void Item_TimedMetadataTracksChanged(MediaPlaybackItem item, IVectorChangedEventArgs args)
319.         {
320.             Log($"item.TimedMetadataTracksChanged: CollectionChange:{args.CollectionChange} Index:{args.Index} Total:{item.TimedMetadataTracks.Count}");
321. 
322.             if (args.CollectionChange == CollectionChange.ItemInserted)
323.             {
324.                 RegisterMetadataHandlers(item, (int)args.Index);
325.             }
326.             if (args.CollectionChange == CollectionChange.Reset)
327.             {
328.                 for (int index = 0; index < item.TimedMetadataTracks.Count; index++)
329.                 {
330.                     RegisterMetadataHandlers(item, index);
331.                 }
332.             }
333.         }
334. 
335.         private void RegisterMetadataHandlers(MediaPlaybackItem mediaPlaybackItem, int index)
336.         {
337.             var timedTrack = mediaPlaybackItem.TimedMetadataTracks[index];
338.             object customValue = null;
339.             mediaPlaybackItem.Source.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("contentId", out customValue);
340.             string contentId = (string)customValue;
341. 
342.             StringBuilder logMsg = new StringBuilder($"{contentId} ");
343. 
344.             if (timedTrack.TrackKind == MediaTrackKind.TimedMetadata &&
345.                 timedTrack.TimedMetadataKind == TimedMetadataKind.Custom)
346.             {
347.                 // Check for our Custom TimedMetadataTrack
348.                 if (mediaPlaybackItem.TimedMetadataTracks[index].Id == "TrackingEvents")
349.                 {
350.                     timedTrack.CueEntered += metadata_TrackingEvents_CueEntered;
351.                     timedTrack.Label = "Tracking Events";
352.                     mediaPlaybackItem.TimedMetadataTracks.SetPresentationMode((uint)index, TimedMetadataTrackPresentationMode.ApplicationPresented);
353.                 }
354. 
355.                 logMsg.AppendLine($"Registered CueEntered for {timedTrack.Label}.");
356.             }
357.             else
358.             {
359.                 logMsg.AppendLine("Did not register CueEntered for TimedMetadataTrack.");
360.             }
361.             logMsg.AppendLine($"TimedMetadataKind: {timedTrack.TimedMetadataKind}, Id: {timedTrack.Id}, Label: {timedTrack.Label}, DispatchType: {timedTrack.DispatchType}, Language: {timedTrack.Language}");
362. 
363.             Log(logMsg.ToString());
364.         }
365. 
366.         private void UnregisterMetadataHandlers(TimedMetadataTrack timedTrack)
367.         {
368.             if (timedTrack.Label == "Tracking Events")
369.             {
370.                 timedTrack.CueEntered -= metadata_TrackingEvents_CueEntered;
371.             }
372.         }
373. 
374.         private void metadata_TrackingEvents_CueEntered(TimedMetadataTrack timedMetadataTrack, MediaCueEventArgs args)
375.         {
376.             StringBuilder logMsg = new StringBuilder();
377.             object customValue = null;
378.             timedMetadataTrack.PlaybackItem.Source.CustomProperties.TryGetValue("contentId", out customValue);
379.             string contentId = (string)customValue;
380.             logMsg.AppendLine($"{contentId} TrackingEventCue CueEntered raised.");
381.             var trackingEventCue = args.Cue as TrackingEventCue;
382.             if (trackingEventCue != null)
383.             {
384.                 logMsg.AppendLine($"Label: {timedMetadataTrack.Label}, Id: {trackingEventCue.Id}, StartTime: {trackingEventCue.StartTime}, Duration: {trackingEventCue.Duration}");
385.                 logMsg.Append($"{trackingEventCue.TrackingEventUri}");
386.                 // TODO: Use the reporing Uri.
387.             }
388.             Log(logMsg.ToString());
389.         }
390.         #endregion

31.     public sealed partial class Scenario4_Tuning : Page
32.     {
33.         CancellationTokenSource ctsForInboundBitsPerSecondUiRefresh = new CancellationTokenSource();
34.         private AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource;
35.         private BitrateHelper bitrateHelper;
36.

43.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             ctsForInboundBitsPerSecondUiRefresh.Cancel(); // Cancel timer
46.             adaptiveMediaSource = null; // release app instance of AdaptiveMediaSource
47. 
48.             // Release handles on various media objects to ensure a quick clean-up
49.             ContentSelectorControl.MediaPlaybackItem = null;
50.             var mediaPlayer = mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer;
51.             if (mediaPlayer != null)
52.             {
53.                 mediaPlayerLogger?.Dispose();
54.                 mediaPlayerLogger = null;
55. 
56.                 UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayer);
57. 
58.                 mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(null);
59.                 mediaPlayer.Dispose();
60.             }
61.         }
62. 
63.         private void UnregisterHandlers(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
64.         {
65.             AdaptiveMediaSource adaptiveMediaSource = null;
66.             MediaPlaybackItem mpItem = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaPlaybackItem;
67.             if (mpItem != null)
68.             {
69.                 adaptiveMediaSource = mpItem.Source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
70.             }
71.             MediaSource source = mediaPlayer.Source as MediaSource;
72.             if (source != null)
73.             {
74.                 adaptiveMediaSource = source.AdaptiveMediaSource;
75.             }
76. 
77.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger?.Dispose();
78.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = null;
79. 
80.             mediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
81.             mediaSourceLogger = null;
82. 
83.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger?.Dispose();
84.             adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = null;
85. 
86.             UnregisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private void Page_OnLoaded(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
90.         {
91.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
92. 
93.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlayer:
94.             mediaPlayerLogger = new MediaPlayerLogger(LoggerControl, mediaPlayer);
95. 
96.             // Ensure we have PlayReady support, if the user enters a DASH/PR Uri in the text box:
97.             var prHelper = new PlayReadyHelper(LoggerControl);
98.             prHelper.SetUpProtectionManager(mediaPlayer);
99.             mediaPlayerElement.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);
100. 
101.             ContentSelectorControl.Initialize(
102.                 mediaPlayer,
103.                 MainPage.ContentManagementSystemStub.Where(m => !m.Aes),
104.                 null,
105.                 LoggerControl,
106.                 LoadSourceFromUriAsync);
107. 
108.             // There is no InboundBitsPerSecondChanged event, so we start a polling thread to update UI.
109.             PollForInboundBitsPerSecond(ctsForInboundBitsPerSecondUiRefresh);
110.         }
111. 
112.         private async void PollForInboundBitsPerSecond(CancellationTokenSource cts)
113.         {
114.             ulong InboundBitsPerSecondLast = 0;
115.             var refreshRate = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(2);
116.             while (!cts.IsCancellationRequested)
117.             {
118.                 if (adaptiveMediaSource != null)
119.                 {
120.                     if (InboundBitsPerSecondLast != adaptiveMediaSource.InboundBitsPerSecond)
121.                     {
122.                         InboundBitsPerSecondLast = adaptiveMediaSource.InboundBitsPerSecond;
123.                         InboundBitsPerSecondText.Text = InboundBitsPerSecondLast.ToString();
124.                     }
125.                 }
126.                 await Task.Delay(refreshRate);
127.             }
128.         }
129. 
130. 
131.         #region Content Loading
132. 
133.         private async Task<MediaPlaybackItem> LoadSourceFromUriAsync(Uri uri, HttpClient httpClient = null)
134.         {
135.             UnregisterHandlers(mediaPlayerElement.MediaPlayer);
136. 
137.             AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationResult result = null;
138.             if (httpClient != null)
139.             {
140.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri, httpClient);
141.             }
142.             else
143.             {
144.                 result = await AdaptiveMediaSource.CreateFromUriAsync(uri);
145.             }
146. 
147.             MediaSource source;
148.             if (result.Status == AdaptiveMediaSourceCreationStatus.Success)
149.             {
150.                 adaptiveMediaSource = result.MediaSource;
151. 
152.                 // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the AdaptiveMediaSource:
153.                 adaptiveMediaSourceLogger = new AdaptiveMediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, adaptiveMediaSource);
154. 
155.                 // In addition to logging, we use the callbacks to update some UI elements in this scenario:
156.                 RegisterForAdaptiveMediaSourceEvents(adaptiveMediaSource);
157. 
158.                 // At this point, we have read the manifest of the media source, and all bitrates are known.
159.                 bitrateHelper = new BitrateHelper(adaptiveMediaSource.AvailableBitrates);
160.                 InitializeBitrateLists(adaptiveMediaSource);
161.                 await UpdatePlaybackBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentPlaybackBitrate);
162.                 await UpdateDownloadBitrateAsync(adaptiveMediaSource.CurrentDownloadBitrate);
163. 
164.                 source = MediaSource.CreateFromAdaptiveMediaSource(adaptiveMediaSource);
165.             }
166.             else
167.             {
168.                 Log($"Error creating the AdaptiveMediaSource. Status: {result.Status}, ExtendedError.Message: {result.ExtendedError.Message}, ExtendedError.HResult: {result.ExtendedError.HResult.ToString("X8")}");
169.                 return null;
170.             }
171. 
172.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaSource:
173.             mediaSourceLogger = new MediaSourceLogger(LoggerControl, source);
174. 
175.             // Save the original Uri.
176.             source.CustomProperties["uri"] = uri.ToString();
177. 
178.             // You're likely to put a content tracking id into the CustomProperties.
179.             source.CustomProperties["contentId"] = Guid.NewGuid().ToString();
180. 
181.             var mpItem = new MediaPlaybackItem(source);
182. 
183.             // We use a helper class that logs all the events for the MediaPlaybackItem:
184.             mediaPlaybackItemLogger = new MediaPlaybackItemLogger(LoggerControl, mpItem);
185. 
186.             HideDescriptionOnSmallScreen();
187. 
188.             return mpItem;
189.         }
190.

194.             // TODO[migrate-009]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
195.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
196.             {
197.                 DescriptionText.Visibility = (ActualHeight < 500) ? Visibility.Collapsed : Visibility.Visible;
198.             });
199.         }

205.         private void InitializeBitrateLists(AdaptiveMediaSource aMS)
206.         {
207.             var sortedBitrates = aMS.AvailableBitrates.OrderByDescending(br => br).Select(br => new BitrateItem(br)).ToArray();
208.             InitialBitrateList.ItemsSource = sortedBitrates;
209.             var selected = sortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
210.             InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = sortedBitrates.FirstOrDefault(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.InitialBitrate);
211. 
212.             var nullableSortedBitrates = (new BitrateItem[] { new BitrateItem(null) }).Concat(sortedBitrates).ToArray();
213.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.ItemsSource = DesiredMinBitrateList.ItemsSource = nullableSortedBitrates;
214. 
215.             DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMaxBitrate);
216.             DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = nullableSortedBitrates.First(item => item.Bitrate == aMS.DesiredMinBitrate);
217.         }
218. 
219.         // An argument exception will be thrown if the following constraint is not met:
220.         //   DesiredMinBitrate <= InitialBitrate <= DesiredMaxBitrate
221.         private bool IsValidBitrateCombination(uint? desiredMinBitrate, uint? desiredMaxBitrate, uint initialBitrate)
222.         {
223.             // The ">" operator returns false if either operand is null. We take advantage of this
224.             // by testing in this manner. Do NOT "optimize" this by reversing the sense of the test
225.             // to "return desiredMinBitrate <= initialBitrate && initialBitrate <= desiredMaxBitrate;"
226.             if (desiredMinBitrate > initialBitrate || initialBitrate > desiredMaxBitrate)
227.             {
228.                 return false;
229.             }
230.             return true;
231.         }
232. 
233.         private void InitialBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
234.         {
235.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
236.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
237.             {
238.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, selection.Bitrate.Value))
239.                 {
240.                     adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate = selection.Bitrate.Value;
241.                 }
242.                 else
243.                 {
244.                     InitialBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
245.                 }
246.             }
247.         }
248. 
249.         private void DesiredMinBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
250.         {
251.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
252.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
253.             {
254.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
255.                 {
256.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
257.                 }
258.                 else
259.                 {
260.                     DesiredMinBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
261.                 }
262.             }
263.         }
264. 
265.         private void DesiredMaxBitrateList_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
266.         {
267.             var selection = (e.AddedItems.Count > 0) ? (BitrateItem)e.AddedItems[0] : null;
268.             if (selection != null && adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate != selection.Bitrate)
269.             {
270.                 if (IsValidBitrateCombination(adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMinBitrate, selection.Bitrate, adaptiveMediaSource.InitialBitrate))
271.                 {
272.                     adaptiveMediaSource.DesiredMaxBitrate = selection.Bitrate;
273.                 }
274.                 else
275.                 {
276.                     DesiredMaxBitrateList.SelectedItem = e.RemovedItems[0];
277.                 }
278.             }
279.         }

364.     /// <summary>
365.     /// Item which provides a nicer display name for "null" bitrate.
366.     /// </summary>
367.     class BitrateItem
368.     {
369.         public uint? Bitrate { get; private set; }
370.         public BitrateItem(uint? bitrate)
371.         {
372.             Bitrate = bitrate;
373.         }
374. 
375.         public override string ToString()
376.         {
377.             return (Bitrate == null) ? "Not set" : Bitrate.ToString();
378.         }
379.     }

426.         /// Enforces a fictitious content publisher's rules for bandwidth maximums based on HDCP protection levels.
427.         /// </summary>
428.         /// <param name="protection">Protection level to use when imposing bandwidth restriction.</param>
429.         /// <param name="ams">AdaptiveMediaSource on which to impose restrictions</param>
430.         private async void SetMaxBitrateForProtectionLevel(HdcpProtection? protection, AdaptiveMediaSource ams)
431.         {
432.             // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
433.             await this.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
434.             {
435.                 EffectiveHdcpProtectionText.Text = protection.ToString();
436. 
437.                 if (ams != null && ams.AvailableBitrates.Count > 1)
438.                 {
439.                     // Get a sorted list of available bitrates.
440.                     var bitrates = new List<uint>(ams.AvailableBitrates);
441.                     bitrates.Sort();
442. 
443.                     // Apply maximum bitrate policy based on a fictitious content publisher's rules.
444.                     switch (protection)
445.                     {
446.                         case HdcpProtection.OnWithTypeEnforcement:
447.                             // Allow full bitrate.
448.                             ams.DesiredMaxBitrate = bitrates[bitrates.Count - 1];
449.                             DesiredMaxBitrateText.Text = "full bitrate allowed";
450.                             break;
451.                         case HdcpProtection.On:
452.                             // When there is no HDCP Type 1, make the highest bitrate unavailable.
453.                             ams.DesiredMaxBitrate = bitrates[bitrates.Count - 2];
454.                             DesiredMaxBitrateText.Text = "highest bitrate is unavailable";
455.                             break;
456.                         case HdcpProtection.Off:
457.                         case null:
458.                         default:
459.                             // When there is no HDCP at all (Off), or the system is still trying to determine what
460.                             // HDCP protection level to apply (null), then make only the lowest bitrate available.
461.                             ams.DesiredMaxBitrate = bitrates[0];
462.                             DesiredMaxBitrateText.Text = "lowest bitrate only";
463.                             break;
464.                     }
465.                     Log($"Imposed DesiredMaxBitrate={ams.DesiredMaxBitrate} for HdcpProtection.{protection}");
466.                 }
467.             });
468.         }
469.         #endregion

12. using System;
13. using System.Linq;
14. using Windows.ApplicationModel.DataTransfer;
15. using Windows.UI;
16. using Windows.UI.Core;
17. using Windows.UI.Xaml;
18. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
19. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Media;
20. 
21. namespace SDKTemplate.Logging
22. {
23.     /// <summary>
24.     /// Logging levels supported by the LogView control.
25.     /// The "Always" level is supported only by the Log method.
26.     /// </summary>
27.     public enum LogViewLoggingLevel
28.     {
29.         Verbose = 0,
30.         Information,
31.         Warning,
32.         Error,
33.         Critical,
34.         Always,
35.     }
36. 
37.     /// 
38.     /// <summary>
39.     /// This control can be used to log events from a scenario page.
40.     /// 
41.     /// In the XAML markup, you can set the initial logging level by setting
42.     /// LoggingLevel="Verbose", or any other enum value of LogViewLoggingLevel.
43.     /// </summary>
44.     public sealed partial class LogView : UserControl
45.     {
46.         public LogView()
47.         {
48.             this.InitializeComponent();
49. 
50.             LoggingBrushes = new SolidColorBrush[] {
51.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["VerboseLogBrush"],
52.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["InformationLogBrush"],
53.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["WarningLogBrush"],
54.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["ErrorLogBrush"],
55.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["CriticalLogBrush"],
56.                 (SolidColorBrush)Resources["AlwaysLogBrush"],
57.             };
58. 
59.             LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)LoggingLevel;
60.         }
61. 
62.         public static readonly DependencyProperty LogLevelProperty =
63.             DependencyProperty.Register("LoggingLevel", typeof(LogViewLoggingLevel), typeof(LogView),
64.                 new PropertyMetadata(LogViewLoggingLevel.Warning, OnLoggingLevelChanged));
65. 
66.         public LogViewLoggingLevel LoggingLevel
67.         {
68.             get { return (LogViewLoggingLevel)GetValue(LogLevelProperty); }
69.             set { SetValue(LogLevelProperty, value); }
70.         }
71. 
72.         private static void OnLoggingLevelChanged(DependencyObject d, DependencyPropertyChangedEventArgs e)
73.         {
74.             var logger = (LogView)d;
75.             var loggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)e.NewValue;
76.             logger.LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = (int)loggingLevel;
77.         }
78. 
79.         bool IsLogViewFull = false;
80.         int MaxItemsInLogView = 500;
81. 
82.         SolidColorBrush[] LoggingBrushes;
83. 
84.         /// <summary>
85.         /// Changes the logging level at which messages are filtered.
86.         /// </summary>
87.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
88.         /// <param name="e"></param>
89.         private void LogLevel_Changed(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
90.         {
91.             LoggingLevel = (LogViewLoggingLevel)LogLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex;
92.         }
93. 
94.         /// <summary>
95.         /// Provides logging with filtering based on UI settings.
96.         /// Logging functions tangential to main scenarios should specify a log view level less than Always.
97.         /// </summary>
98.         /// <param name="message">message to be logged</param>
99.         /// <param name="level">message category</param>
100.         public async void Log(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level = LogViewLoggingLevel.Always)
101.         {
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 LogFromUIThread(message, level);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
109.                 {
110.                     LogFromUIThread(message, level);
111.                 });
112.             }
113.         }
114. 
115.         private void LogFromUIThread(string message, LogViewLoggingLevel level)
116.         {
117.             string logEntry = $"{DateTime.Now:HH:mm:ss.fff} - {message}";
118. 
119.             if (LoggingLevel <= level)
120.             {
121.                 SolidColorBrush foregroundBrush = LoggingBrushes[(int)level];
122. 
123.                 if (IsLogViewFull)
124.                 {
125.                     // Once the view is full, messages go only to the debug console.
126.                     System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);
127.                     return;
128.                 }
129. 
130.                 if (LoggingListBox.Items.Count >= MaxItemsInLogView)
131.                 {
132.                     IsLogViewFull = true;
133. 
134.                     System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine(logEntry);
135.                     logEntry = "Log window is full";
136.                     foregroundBrush = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Red);
137.                 }
138. 
139.                 LoggingListBox.Items.Insert(0, new TextBlock() { Text = logEntry, Foreground = foregroundBrush });
140.             }
141.             else if (level != LogViewLoggingLevel.Verbose)
142.             {
143.                 // Non-Verbose messages blocked from the LogView are shown on the debug console.
144.                 System.Diagnostics.Debug.WriteLine($"{level}: {logEntry}");
145.             }
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void CopyLogToClipboard_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
149.         {
150.             var content = new DataPackage();
151.             content.SetText(String.Join("\r\n", LoggingListBox.Items.Select(item => (item as TextBlock).Text)));
152.             Clipboard.SetContent(content);
153.         }
154. 
155.         private void ClearLog_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
156.         {
157.             ClearLog();
158.         }
159. 
160.         public async void ClearLog()
161.         {
162.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
163.             {
164.                 LoggingListBox.Items.Clear();
165.                 IsLogViewFull = false;
166.             }
167.             else
168.             {
169.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, ClearLog);
170.             }
171.         }
172.     }
173. }

{"command":"& \"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run35\\000_AdaptiveStreaming\\app\\.github\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Get-MigrationPattern.ps1\" -Anchor \"startup-crashes\"","description":"Get startup-crashes migration pattern"}

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup CrashesA packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.
<exited with exit code 0>

71.         public void SetUpProtectionManager(MediaPlayer mediaPlayer)
72.         {
73.             Log("Enter SetUpProtectionManager");
74. 
75.             if(mediaPlayer == null)
76.                 throw new ArgumentException("SetUpProtectionManager was passed a null MediaPlayer");
77.             
78.             Log("Creating protection system mappings...");
79.             var protectionManager = new MediaProtectionManager();
80. 
81.             protectionManager.ComponentLoadFailed += new ComponentLoadFailedEventHandler(ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed);
82.             protectionManager.ServiceRequested += new ServiceRequestedEventHandler(ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested);
83. 
84.             // The code here is mandatory and should be just copied directly over to the app
85.             // Ref: https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/windows/desktop/aa376846%28v=vs.85%29.aspx
86. 
87.             // Setup PlayReady as the ProtectionSystem to use by mediaFoundation: 
88.             var contentProtectionSystems = new PropertySet();
89.             contentProtectionSystems.Add(PlayReadyWinRTTrustedInput);
90.             protectionManager.Properties.Add(MediaProtectionSystemIdMapping, contentProtectionSystems);
91.             protectionManager.Properties.Add(MediaProtectionSystemId);
92.             protectionManager.Properties.Add(MediaProtectionContainerGuid);
93. 
94.             mediaPlayer.ProtectionManager = protectionManager;
95. 
96.             Log("Leave SetUpProtectionManager");
97.         }
98. 
99.         private async void ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested(MediaProtectionManager sender, ServiceRequestedEventArgs e)
100.         {
101.             Log("Enter ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested");
102. 
103.             if (e.Request is PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest)
104.             {
105.                 var individualizationRequest = e.Request as PlayReadyIndividualizationServiceRequest;
106.                 bool bResultIndiv = await ReactiveIndividualizationRequestAsync(individualizationRequest, e.Completion);
107.             }
108.             else if (e.Request is PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest)
109.             {
110.                 var licenseRequest = e.Request as PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest;
111.                 LicenseAcquisitionRequest(licenseRequest, e.Completion, playReadyLicenseUrl, playReadyChallengeCustomData);
112.             }
113. 
114.             Log("Leave ProtectionManager_ServiceRequested");
115.         }
116. 
117.         private void ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed(MediaProtectionManager sender, ComponentLoadFailedEventArgs e)
118.         {
119.             Log("Enter ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed");
120.             Log(e.Information.ToString());
121. 
122.             // List the failing components - RevocationAndRenewalInformation
123.             foreach (var item in e.Information.Items)
124.             {
125.                 Log(item.Name + 
126.                     "\nReasons=0x" + item.Reasons + 
127.                     "\nRenewal Id=" + item.RenewalId);
128.             }
129.             e.Completion.Complete(false);
130. 
131.             Log("Leave ProtectionManager_ComponentLoadFailed");
132.         }

134.         /// <summary>
135.         /// Invoked to acquire the PlayReady License
136.         /// </summary>
137.         async void LicenseAcquisitionRequest(PlayReadyLicenseAcquisitionServiceRequest licenseRequest, MediaProtectionServiceCompletion completionNotifier, string url, string challengeCustomData)
138.         {
139.             bool bResult = false;
140.             string ExceptionMessage = string.Empty;
141. 
142.             try
143.             {
144.                 if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(url))
145.                 {
146.                     Log("ProtectionManager PlayReady Manual License Acquisition Service Request in progress - URL: " + url);
147. 
148.                     if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(challengeCustomData))
149.                     {
150.                         var encoding = new UTF8Encoding();
151.                         byte[] b = encoding.GetBytes(challengeCustomData);
152.                         licenseRequest.ChallengeCustomData = Convert.ToBase64String(b, 0, b.Length);
153.                     }
154. 
155.                     PlayReadySoapMessage soapMessage = licenseRequest.GenerateManualEnablingChallenge();
156. 
157.                     byte[] messageBytes = soapMessage.GetMessageBody();
158.                     HttpContent httpContent = new ByteArrayContent(messageBytes);
159. 
160.                     IPropertySet propertySetHeaders = soapMessage.MessageHeaders;
161.                     foreach (string strHeaderName in propertySetHeaders.Keys)
162.                     {
163.                         string strHeaderValue = propertySetHeaders[strHeaderName].ToString();
164. 
165.                         // The Add method throws an ArgumentException try to set protected headers like "Content-Type"
166.                         // so set it via "ContentType" property
167.                         if (strHeaderName.Equals("Content-Type", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase))
168.                             httpContent.Headers.ContentType = MediaTypeHeaderValue.Parse(strHeaderValue);
169.                         else
170.                             httpContent.Headers.Add(strHeaderName.ToString(), strHeaderValue);
171.                     }
172.                     var licenseAcquision = new CommonLicenseRequest();
173.                     HttpContent responseHttpContent = await licenseAcquision.AcquireLicenseAsync(new Uri(url), httpContent);
174.                     if (responseHttpContent != null)
175.                     {
176.                         Exception exResult = licenseRequest.ProcessManualEnablingResponse(await responseHttpContent.ReadAsByteArrayAsync());
177.                         if (exResult != null)
178.                         {
179.                             throw exResult;
180.                         }

{"path":"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run35\\000_AdaptiveStreaming\\app\\.github\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Test-AppLaunch.ps1"}
--- RESULT ---
1. <#
2. .SYNOPSIS
3. Launch an already-built WinUI 3 app and report whether it survives startup -
4. and if not, capture the REAL unhandled-exception signature from Windows Error
5. Reporting so a startup crash can be diagnosed immediately instead of guessed at.
6. 
7. .DESCRIPTION
8. A packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash on startup. When that
9. happens the only durable signal is the Windows Application event log: event 1000
10. (faulting module + native exception code) and event 1026 (.NET Runtime - the
11. managed exception TYPE, message, and stack). This script launches the built
12. layout via 'winapp run --detach --json', waits for the cold-start window, and:
13. 
14.   - if the process is alive  -> status=running  (exit 0)
15.   - if it registered then died/threw at startup -> status=crashed (exit 1),
16.     with the captured WER signature + a pointer to PATTERNS.md#startup-crashes
17.   - if it never registered (deploy/env failure: Developer Mode off, missing
18.     framework, MAX_PATH, no winapp) -> status=unavailable (exit 2) - inconclusive,
19.     NOT a code defect.
20. 
21. It NEVER throws on an operational failure; it returns a structured result and
22. (with -Json) prints it as JSON. This is the single source of truth for the
23. "did the migrated app launch" question - both Step 3 (interactive, when the app
24. first crashes) and the Step 4 validator (Section 7) call it.
25. 
26. WHY THIS MATTERS: the common failure mode is an agent that sees a startup crash
27. and hand-rolls File.WriteAllText tracing for the rest of its budget. Don't. Run
28. this once; read the captured managed exception; fix the named frame.
29. 
30. NOTE: this script is intentionally ASCII-only. The Step 4 validator invokes it
31. via 'powershell -File' (Windows PowerShell 5.1), which reads a UTF-8-no-BOM file
32. as ANSI; non-ASCII characters would corrupt parsing under 5.1.
33. 
34. .PARAMETER Target
35. The migrated WinUI 3 project root (the folder with the .csproj). The build-output
36. layout is discovered under bin/<arch>/Debug/<tfm>/win-<rid>. Use this OR -Layout.
37. 
38. .PARAMETER Layout
39. An explicit build-output layout folder (the one containing AppxManifest.xml +
40. the app .exe, or an AppX\ subfolder). Use this OR -Target.
41. 
42. .PARAMETER SettleSeconds
43. Seconds to wait after launch before the alive check. Default 10 - enough to
44. catch OnLaunched / first-Page-navigation crashes without bloating runtime.
45. 
46. .PARAMETER Json
47. Emit the structured result as JSON to stdout (for programmatic callers).
48. 
49. .OUTPUTS
50. PSCustomObject (and JSON with -Json):
51.   { ok, status, pid, aumid, layout, crash:{ code, module, managedType, message,
52.     hint, anchor } | $null, detail }
53. status in { running, crashed, unavailable }.  exit: 0 running / 1 crashed / 2 unavailable.
54. 
55. .EXAMPLE
56. .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Target "C:\out\MyWinUI3App"
57. # Interactive: launch and, on crash, print the real exception + the fix pointer.
58. 
59. .EXAMPLE
60. $r = .\Test-AppLaunch.ps1 -Layout $launchFolder -Json | ConvertFrom-Json
61. if ($r.status -eq 'crashed') { "FAIL: $($r.crash.hint)" }
62. #>
63. [CmdletBinding(DefaultParameterSetName = 'Target')]
64. param(
65.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Target')][string]$Target,
66.     [Parameter(Mandatory, ParameterSetName = 'Layout')][string]$Layout,
67.     [int]$SettleSeconds = 10,
68.     [switch]$Json
69. )
70. 
71. # This script reports failures structurally; it must not abort on a non-zero
72. # external tool exit. Errors are handled explicitly per stage.
73. $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
74. 
75. function New-LaunchResult {
76.     param([bool]$Ok, [string]$Status, [string]$Detail, [hashtable]$Extra)
77.     $r = [ordered]@{
78.         ok = $Ok; status = $Status; pid = $null; aumid = $null
79.         layout = $null; crash = $null; detail = $Detail
80.     }
81.     if ($Extra) { foreach ($k in $Extra.Keys) { $r[$k] = $Extra[$k] } }
82.     [pscustomobject]$r
83. }
84. 
85. function Write-LaunchOut {
86.     param([pscustomobject]$Result)
87.     # exit code: running=0, crashed=1, unavailable=2
88.     $exit = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 0 } 'crashed' { 1 } default { 2 } }
89.     if ($Json) {
90.         $Result | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 6
91.     } else {
92.         $tag = switch ($Result.status) { 'running' { 'OK  ' } 'crashed' { 'FAIL' } default { 'WARN' } }
93.         Write-Host ""
94.         Write-Host "==> Test-AppLaunch [$tag] status=$($Result.status)"
95.         if ($Result.layout) { Write-Host "    Layout : $($Result.layout)" }
96.         if ($Result.pid)    { Write-Host "    PID    : $($Result.pid)   AUMID: $($Result.aumid)" }
97.         if ($Result.crash) {
98.             Write-Host "    Crash  : code=$($Result.crash.code)  module=$($Result.crash.module)"
99.             if ($Result.crash.managedType) { Write-Host "    .NET   : $($Result.crash.managedType)" }
100.             if ($Result.crash.message)     { Write-Host "    Message: $($Result.crash.message)" }
101.             if ($Result.crash.hint)        { Write-Host "    Hint   : $($Result.crash.hint)" }
102.             if ($Result.crash.anchor)      { Write-Host "    Fix    : Get-MigrationPattern.ps1 -Anchor $($Result.crash.anchor)" }
103.         }
104.         if ($Result.detail) { Write-Host "    Detail : $($Result.detail)" }
105.     }
106.     exit $exit
107. }
108. 
109. # ---- Resolve the launch layout ----------------------------------------------
110. $exeNames = @()
111. if ($PSCmdlet.ParameterSetName -eq 'Target') {
112.     if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Target)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Target not found: $Target") }
113.     $Target = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Target).Path
114.     $csproj = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Target -Recurse -Filter *.csproj -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
115.         Where-Object { $_.FullName -notmatch '\\(bin|obj|\.uwp-source)\\' } | Select-Object -First 1 -ExpandProperty FullName
116.     if (-not $csproj) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No .csproj found under $Target") }
117.     $csprojDir = Split-Path -Parent $csproj
118.     $arch = if ($env:PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE -eq 'ARM64') { 'ARM64' } else { 'x64' }
119.     $rid = $arch.ToLower()
120.     # Walk bin/<arch>/Debug and one level into the newest TFM dir, then win-<rid>.
121.     $binCandidates = @(
122.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$arch\Debug"),
123.         (Join-Path $csprojDir "bin\$rid\Debug")
124.     )
125.     foreach ($bin in $binCandidates) {
126.         if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $bin)) { continue }
127.         $tfmDir = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $bin -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
128.             Sort-Object LastWriteTime -Descending | Select-Object -First 1
129.         if (-not $tfmDir) { continue }
130.         $ridDir = Join-Path $tfmDir.FullName "win-$rid"
131.         $Layout = if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $ridDir) { $ridDir } else { $tfmDir.FullName }
132.         break
133.     }
134.     if (-not $Layout) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "No build output found under bin\$arch\Debug - build the project first.") }
135. }
136. 
137. if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Layout)) { Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout folder not found: $Layout") }
138. $Layout = (Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $Layout).Path
139. 
140. # Sanity: the layout needs an exe + a manifest, else winapp run can't launch it.
141. $exes = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Layout -Filter '*.exe' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
142. $exeNames = @($exes | ForEach-Object { [System.IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($_.Name) })
143. $hasMfst = (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppxManifest.xml')) -or
144.            (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppX\AppxManifest.xml'))
145. if ($exes.Count -eq 0 -or -not $hasMfst) {
146.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout is incomplete (need an .exe + AppxManifest.xml): $Layout" @{ layout = $Layout })
147. }
148. if (-not (Get-Command winapp -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
149.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "winapp CLI not on PATH - cannot launch the packaged app." @{ layout = $Layout })
150. }
151. 
152. # ---- Launch ------------------------------------------------------------------
153. $runRaw = & winapp run "$Layout" --detach --json 2>&1 | Out-String
154. $run = $null
155. try { $run = $runRaw.Trim() | ConvertFrom-Json -ErrorAction Stop } catch { }
156. $thePid = $null; $aumid = $null; $runErr = $null
157. if ($run) {
158.     foreach ($p in 'ProcessId','PID','Pid') { if ($run.$p) { $thePid = [int]$run.$p; break } }
159.     if ($run.AUMID) { $aumid = [string]$run.AUMID }
160.     if ($run.Error) { $runErr = [string]$run.Error }
161. }
162. 
163. # ---- WER capture helper ------------------------------------------------------
164. # Reads the real unhandled-exception signature for the just-crashed app:
165. #   event 1000  -> faulting module + native Exception code (e.g. 0x8001010E)
166. #   event 1026  -> .NET Runtime: managed exception TYPE + message + stack
167. # Match on the layout's exe base name(s) within a short recent window.
168. function Get-CrashSignature {
169.     param([string[]]$Names)
170.     $sig = [ordered]@{ code = $null; module = $null; managedType = $null; message = $null; hint = $null; anchor = $null }
171.     if (-not $Names -or $Names.Count -eq 0) { return $sig }
172.     $namePattern = ($Names | ForEach-Object { [regex]::Escape($_ + '.exe') }) -join '|'
173.     $events = Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 80 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
174.         Where-Object { $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) -and $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.Message -match $namePattern }
175. 
176.     $e1000 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1000 } | Select-Object -First 1
177.     if ($e1000) {
178.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Faulting module name:\s*([^,]+)') { $sig.module = $matches[1].Trim() }
179.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Exception code:\s*(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)') { $sig.code = $matches[1].ToLower() }
180.     }
181.     $e1026 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1026 } | Select-Object -First 1
182.     if ($e1026) {
183.         # ".NET Runtime" event: "Exception Info: <Type>: <message>"
184.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception)') { $sig.managedType = $matches[1].Trim() }
185.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*[A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception[^\r\n:]*:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
186.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
187.         } elseif ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
188.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
189.         }
190.     }
191. 
192.     # Map the native exception code to a conservative hint + pattern anchor.
193.     # These name the error CLASS and point at the captured stack / the doc; they
194.     # do not assert a single fix, since the real frame is in the captured exception.
195.     switch ($sig.code) {
196.         '0x80004003' {
197.             $sig.hint   = "E_POINTER - most often the static-window init-order race: a Page read App.MainWindow (or another static window ref) before OnLaunched assigned it. Keep MainWindow's ctor inert; navigate after Activate."
198.             $sig.anchor = 'windowing'
199.         }
200.         '0x8001010e' {
201.             $sig.hint   = "RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD - a thread/apartment-affined object was accessed during startup (commonly a view/CoreWindow-affined UWP API touched from a static initializer or off the UI thread). Construct/access it on the UI thread after Activate; if the API is unsupported on WinUI 3 desktop, defer it."
202.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
203.         }
204.         '0xe0434352' {
205.             $sig.hint   = "Managed .NET exception - see the .NET exception type captured above (event 1026). Resolve that type (e.g. TypeLoad / FileNotFound usually means a missing or incompatible package reference)."
206.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
207.         }
208.         '0xc000027b' {
209.             $sig.hint   = "Native stowed exception - frequently a legacy projection/activation incompatibility. If a UWP API/contract used at startup is unsupported on this OS, defer it per MIGRATION-DEFERRED.md."
210.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
211.         }
212.         default {
213.             $sig.hint   = "Startup crash - read the captured exception (event 1026) to find the throwing frame."
214.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
215.         }
216.     }
217.     return $sig
218. }
219. 
220. # ---- Classify ----------------------------------------------------------------
221. if ($thePid) {
222.     Start-Sleep -Seconds $SettleSeconds
223.     $alive = $null -ne (Get-Process -Id $thePid -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
224.     if ($alive) {
225.         # Best-effort cleanup so we don't leave the dev-registered app running.
226.         try { Stop-Process -Id $thePid -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue } catch {}
227.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $true 'running' "App stayed alive ${SettleSeconds}s after launch (pid $thePid)." @{ layout = $Layout; pid = $thePid; aumid = $aumid })
228.     } else {
229.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
230.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' "App launched (pid $thePid) but exited within ${SettleSeconds}s - startup crash." @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
231.     }
232. } else {
233.     # No PID. Distinguish a startup crash from a deploy/env failure by whether the
234.     # app actually got REGISTERED/ACTIVATED (winapp emits an AUMID once it does).
235.     if ($aumid) {
236.         # Registered + activated, then threw before it could report a PID => startup crash (DEFECT).
237.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
238.         $detail = "App registered (AUMID present) but crashed during activation/startup"
239.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - winapp reported '" + $runErr + "'" }
240.         $detail = $detail + "."
241.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' $detail @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
242.     } else {
243.         # Never registered => deployment/environment problem, not a code defect. Inconclusive.
244.         $detail = "winapp run did not register the app (no AUMID, no PID)"
245.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - '" + $runErr + "'" }
246.         $detail = $detail + ". Usually Developer Mode off, a missing framework dependency, cert issue, or MAX_PATH - not a migration defect."
247.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' $detail @{ layout = $Layout })
248.     }
249. }
250.

Let me try providing a custom `Program.cs` with `[MTAThread]` since WinAppSDK 2.x might require MTA:

--- TOOL: create ---
{"file_text":"// Custom entry point — disables auto-generated Main so we can configure threading\n// explicitly for WinAppSDK 2.x which activates Application from MTA context.\n#define DISABLE_XAML_GENERATED_MAIN\n\nnamespace AdaptiveStreaming;\n\npublic static class Program\n{\n    [global::System.MTAThread]\n    static void Main(string[] args)\n    {\n        global::WinRT.ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers();\n        global::Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start((p) =>\n        {\n            var context = new global::Microsoft.UI.Dispatching.DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext(\n                global::Microsoft.UI.Dispatching.DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread());\n            global::System.Threading.SynchronizationContext.SetSynchronizationContext(context);\n            _ = new App();\n        });\n    }\n}\n","path":"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run35\\000_AdaptiveStreaming\\app\\AdaptiveStreaming\\Program.cs"}
